<p><b>Sint Maarten</b> (<small>Dutch pronunciation:&#32;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="nl-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Dutch" title="Help:IPA/Dutch">[sɪntˈmaːrtə(n)]</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/2\/2f\/Nl-Sint_Maarten.ogg\/Nl-Sint_Maarten.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;Nl-Sint Maarten.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/2/2f/Nl-Sint_Maarten.ogg/Nl-Sint_Maarten.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Nl-Sint_Maarten.ogg" title="File:Nl-Sint Maarten.ogg">i</a></sup>) is a <a href="/wiki/Countries_of_the_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Countries of the Kingdom of the Netherlands">constituent country</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> With a population of 41,486<sup id="cite_ref-rijksoverheid.nl_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rijksoverheid.nl-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> as of January 2019 on an area of 34&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (13&#160;sq&#160;mi), it encompasses the southern 44% of the <a href="/wiki/Divided_island" class="mw-redirect" title="Divided island">divided island</a> of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Martin_(island)" title="Saint Martin (island)">Saint Martin</a>, while the northern 56% of the island constitutes the French <a href="/wiki/Overseas_collectivity" title="Overseas collectivity">overseas collectivity</a> of <a href="/wiki/Collectivity_of_Saint_Martin" title="Collectivity of Saint Martin">Saint Martin</a>. Sint Maarten's <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital</a> is <a href="/wiki/Philipsburg,_Sint_Maarten" title="Philipsburg, Sint Maarten">Philipsburg</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Collectively, Sint Maarten and the other Dutch islands in the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> are often called the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean" title="Dutch Caribbean">Dutch Caribbean</a>.
</p><p>Before 10 October 2010, Sint Maarten was known as the Island Territory of Sint Maarten (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>: <i lang="nl">Eilandgebied Sint Maarten</i>), and was one of six (from 1986 five) <a href="/wiki/Island_territories_of_the_Netherlands_Antilles" title="Island territories of the Netherlands Antilles">island territories</a> (<span title="Dutch-language text"><i lang="nl">eilandgebieden</i></span>) that constituted the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Sint Maarten has the status of an overseas country and territory (OCT) and is not part of the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>.
</p><p>On 6 and 7 September 2017, the island was hit by <a href="/wiki/Saffir%E2%80%93Simpson_scale#Category_5" title="Saffir–Simpson scale">Category 5</a> <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Irma" title="Hurricane Irma">Hurricane Irma</a>, which <a href="#Effects_of_Hurricane_Irma">caused widespread and significant damage</a> to buildings and infrastructure.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The island was named by <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Columbus" title="Christopher Columbus">Christopher Columbus</a> in honour of <a href="/wiki/St_Martin_of_Tours" class="mw-redirect" title="St Martin of Tours">St Martin of Tours</a>, as he first sighted it on the saint's feast day on 11 November 1493.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>"Sint Maarten" is <a href="/wiki/Saint_Martin_(island)" title="Saint Martin (island)">Saint Martin</a> in Dutch.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Saint_Martin" class="mw-redirect" title="History of Saint Martin">History of Saint Martin</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean" title="Dutch Caribbean">Dutch Caribbean</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pre-colonial">Pre-colonial</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Pre-colonial">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Sint Maarten had been inhabited by <a href="/wiki/Amerindian" class="mw-redirect" title="Amerindian">Amerindian</a> peoples for many centuries, with archaeological finds pointing to a human presence on the island as early as 2000 BC.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> These people most likely migrated from South America.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The earliest identified group were the <a href="/wiki/Arawak" title="Arawak">Arawak</a> people who are thought to have settled around the period 800 BC – 300 BC.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> Circa 1300-1400 AD they began to be displaced with the arrival of <a href="/wiki/Kalina_people" title="Kalina people">Carib</a> people.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Arrival_of_Europeans">Arrival of Europeans</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Arrival of Europeans">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Expulsi%C3%B3nDeLosHolandesesDeLaIslaDeSanMart%C3%ADn16330701-2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Expulsi%C3%B3nDeLosHolandesesDeLaIslaDeSanMart%C3%ADn16330701-2.jpg/220px-Expulsi%C3%B3nDeLosHolandesesDeLaIslaDeSanMart%C3%ADn16330701-2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Expulsi%C3%B3nDeLosHolandesesDeLaIslaDeSanMart%C3%ADn16330701-2.jpg/330px-Expulsi%C3%B3nDeLosHolandesesDeLaIslaDeSanMart%C3%ADn16330701-2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Expulsi%C3%B3nDeLosHolandesesDeLaIslaDeSanMart%C3%ADn16330701-2.jpg/440px-Expulsi%C3%B3nDeLosHolandesesDeLaIslaDeSanMart%C3%ADn16330701-2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2972" data-file-height="1996" /></a><figcaption>The 1633 Spanish capture of Saint Martin, as painted by Juan de la Corte</figcaption></figure>
<p>It is commonly believed that <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Columbus" title="Christopher Columbus">Christopher Columbus</a> named the island in honor of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Martin_of_Tours" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Martin of Tours">Saint Martin of Tours</a> when he encountered it on his second voyage of discovery. However, he actually applied the name to the island now called <a href="/wiki/Nevis" title="Nevis">Nevis</a> when he anchored offshore on November 11, 1493, the feast day of Saint Martin. The confusion of numerous poorly charted small islands in the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands" title="Leeward Islands">Leeward Islands</a> meant that this name was accidentally transferred to the island now known as Saint-Martin/Sint Maarten.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nominally Spanish territory, the island became the focus of the competing interest of the European powers, notably France, Britain and the Netherlands. While the French wanted to colonize the islands between <a href="/wiki/Trinidad" title="Trinidad">Trinidad</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bermuda" title="Bermuda">Bermuda</a>, the Dutch found <i>San Martín</i> a convenient halfway point between their colonies in <a href="/wiki/New_Amsterdam" title="New Amsterdam">New Amsterdam</a> (present day <a href="/wiki/New_York_City" title="New York City">New York</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Brazil" title="Dutch Brazil">New Holland</a>. Meanwhile, the Amerindian population began to decline precipitously, dying from introduced diseases to which they had no immunity.
</p><p>The Dutch built a fort (<a href="/wiki/Fort_Amsterdam_(Sint_Maarten)" title="Fort Amsterdam (Sint Maarten)">Fort Amsterdam</a>) on the island in 1631; Jan Claeszen van Campen became its first <a href="/wiki/List_of_Sint_Maarten_governors" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Sint Maarten governors">governor</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_West_India_Company" title="Dutch West India Company">Dutch West India Company</a> began mining <a href="/wiki/Salt" title="Salt">salt</a> on the island. Tensions between the Netherlands and Spain were already high due to the ongoing <a href="/wiki/Eighty_Years%27_War" title="Eighty Years&#39; War">Eighty Years' War</a>, and in 1633 the Spanish <a href="/wiki/Capture_of_Saint_Martin_(1633)" title="Capture of Saint Martin (1633)">captured St Martin</a> and drove off the Dutch colonists. At Point Blanche, they built what is now Old Spanish Fort to secure the territory.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The Dutch under <a href="/wiki/Peter_Stuyvesant" title="Peter Stuyvesant">Peter Stuyvesant</a> attempted to <a href="/wiki/Attack_on_Saint_Martin" title="Attack on Saint Martin">wrest back control</a> in 1644, but were repulsed.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> However, in 1648 the Eighty Years' War ended and the Spanish, no longer seeing any strategic or economic value in the island, simply abandoned it.
</p><p>With Saint Martin free again, both the Dutch and the French jumped at the chance to re-establish their settlements.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> Dutch colonists came from <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">St. Eustatius</a>, while the French came from <a href="/wiki/St._Kitts" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Kitts">St. Kitts</a>. After some initial conflict, both sides realized that neither would yield easily. Preferring to avoid an all-out war, they signed the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Concordia" title="Treaty of Concordia">Treaty of Concordia</a> in 1648, which divided the island in two.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> During the treaty's negotiation, the French had a fleet of <a href="/wiki/Navy" title="Navy">naval</a> ships off shore, which they used as a threat to bargain more land for themselves.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2015)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In spite of the treaty, relations between the two sides were not always cordial. Between 1648 and 1816, conflicts changed the <a href="/wiki/France%E2%80%93Netherlands_border" class="mw-redirect" title="France–Netherlands border">border</a> sixteen times. The entire island came under effective French control from 1795 when Netherlands became a <a href="/wiki/Puppet_state" title="Puppet state">puppet state</a> under the <a href="/wiki/First_French_Empire" title="First French Empire">French Empire</a> until 1815. In the end, the French came out ahead with 53&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (20&#160;sq&#160;mi; 61%) against 34&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (13&#160;sq&#160;mi; 39%) on the Dutch side.
</p>
<h3><span id="18th.E2.80.9319th_centuries"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="18th–19th_centuries">18th–19th centuries</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: 18th–19th centuries">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>To work the new cotton, tobacco and sugar plantations the French and Dutch began importing large numbers of African slaves, who soon came to outnumber the Europeans.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The slave population quickly grew larger than that of the land owners. Subjected to cruel treatment, slaves staged rebellions, and their overwhelming numbers made it impossible to ignore their concerns. In 1848, the French <a href="/wiki/Abolitionism#Second_abolition_(1848)_and_subsequent_events" title="Abolitionism">abolished slavery</a> in their colonies including the French side of St. Martin. Slaves on the Dutch side of the island protested and threatened to flee to the French side to seek asylum. The local Dutch authorities then freed the colonies' slaves. While this decree was respected locally, it was not until 1863 when the Dutch abolished slavery in all of their island colonies that the slaves became legally free.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="20th_century">20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: 20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dr._Claude_Wathey_St._Maarten.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Dr._Claude_Wathey_St._Maarten.JPG/220px-Dr._Claude_Wathey_St._Maarten.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Dr._Claude_Wathey_St._Maarten.JPG/330px-Dr._Claude_Wathey_St._Maarten.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Dr._Claude_Wathey_St._Maarten.JPG/440px-Dr._Claude_Wathey_St._Maarten.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2144" data-file-height="1424" /></a><figcaption>Statue of Claude Wathey in <a href="/wiki/Philipsburg,_Sint_Maarten" title="Philipsburg, Sint Maarten">Philipsburg</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>After the abolition of slavery, plantation culture declined and the island's economy suffered. In 1939 Sint Maarten received a major boost when it was declared a <a href="/wiki/Duty-free" class="mw-redirect" title="Duty-free">duty-free</a> port. In 1941 the island was shelled by a <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">German</a> <a href="/wiki/U-boat" title="U-boat">U-boat</a> as part of the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Atlantic" title="Battle of the Atlantic">Battle of the Atlantic</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Tourism began growing from the 1950s onward, and <a href="/wiki/Princess_Juliana_International_Airport" title="Princess Juliana International Airport">Princess Juliana International Airport</a> became one of the busiest in the Eastern Caribbean. For much of this period, Sint Maarten was governed by business tycoon <a href="/wiki/Claude_Wathey" title="Claude Wathey">Claude Wathey</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_Sint_Maarten" title="Democratic Party Sint Maarten">Democratic Party</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The island's demographics changed dramatically during this period as well, with the population increasing from a mere 5,000 people to around 60,000 people by the mid-1990s. Immigration from the neighbouring Lesser Antilles, <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Curaçao">Curaçao</a>, <a href="/wiki/Haiti" title="Haiti">Haiti</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Dominican_Republic" title="Dominican Republic">Dominican Republic</a>, the United States, Europe, and Asia turned the native population into a minority.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sint Maarten became an "island territory" (<i>eilandgebied</i> in <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>) of the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a> in 1983. Before that date, Sint Maarten was part of the island territory of the Windward Islands, together with <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">Sint Eustatius</a>. The status of an island territory entails considerable autonomy summed up in the <a href="/wiki/Island_Regulation_of_the_Netherlands_Antilles" class="mw-redirect" title="Island Regulation of the Netherlands Antilles">Island Regulation of the Netherlands Antilles</a>. During this period Sint Maarten was ruled by an island council, an executive council, and a lieutenant governor (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>: <i lang="nl">gezaghebber</i>) appointed by the Dutch Crown.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Luis" title="Hurricane Luis">Hurricane Luis</a> in late August and early September 1995 hit the island, causing immense destruction and resulting in 12 deaths.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="21st_century">21st century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: 21st century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1994, the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a> and France had signed the <a href="/wiki/Franco-Dutch_treaty_on_Saint_Martin_border_controls" title="Franco-Dutch treaty on Saint Martin border controls">Franco-Dutch treaty on Saint Martin border controls</a>, which allows for joint Franco-Dutch border controls on so-called "risk flights". After some delay, the treaty was ratified in November 2006 in the Netherlands and subsequently entered into force on 1 August 2007. Though the treaty is now in force, its provisions are not yet<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The time period mentioned near this tag is ambiguous. (February 2015)">when?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> implemented as the working group specified in the treaty is not yet installed.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On 10 October 2010 Sint Maarten became a <a href="/wiki/Countries_of_the_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Countries of the Kingdom of the Netherlands">constituent country</a> (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>: <i lang="nl">Land Sint Maarten</i>) within the Kingdom of the Netherlands, making it a constitutional equal partner with <a href="/wiki/Aruba" title="Aruba">Aruba</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Curaçao">Curaçao</a>, and the Netherlands proper. Constitution Day (10 October) is celebrated annually as a <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Sint_Maarten" title="Public holidays in Sint Maarten">public holiday</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sint Maarten has been assigned the <a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-1_alpha-2" title="ISO 3166-1 alpha-2">ISO 3166-1 alpha-2</a> country codes of SXM and SX,<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/.sx" title=".sx">.sx</a> Internet <a href="/wiki/CcTLD" class="mw-redirect" title="CcTLD">ccTLD</a> became available to register on 15 November 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Effects_of_Hurricane_Irma">Effects of Hurricane Irma</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Effects of Hurricane Irma">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Philipsburg_St_Maarten.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Philipsburg_St_Maarten.jpg/220px-Philipsburg_St_Maarten.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="132" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Philipsburg_St_Maarten.jpg/330px-Philipsburg_St_Maarten.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Philipsburg_St_Maarten.jpg/440px-Philipsburg_St_Maarten.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="300" /></a><figcaption>The port in Sint Maarten before Hurricane Irma</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hurricane_Irma_on_Sint_Maarten_(NL)_05.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Hurricane_Irma_on_Sint_Maarten_%28NL%29_05.jpg/220px-Hurricane_Irma_on_Sint_Maarten_%28NL%29_05.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Hurricane_Irma_on_Sint_Maarten_%28NL%29_05.jpg/330px-Hurricane_Irma_on_Sint_Maarten_%28NL%29_05.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Hurricane_Irma_on_Sint_Maarten_%28NL%29_05.jpg/440px-Hurricane_Irma_on_Sint_Maarten_%28NL%29_05.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1250" data-file-height="833" /></a><figcaption>Damaged buildings in the wake of Hurricane Irma</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:St_Maarten_Hurricane_Damage.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/St_Maarten_Hurricane_Damage.jpg/220px-St_Maarten_Hurricane_Damage.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="178" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/St_Maarten_Hurricane_Damage.jpg/330px-St_Maarten_Hurricane_Damage.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/St_Maarten_Hurricane_Damage.jpg/440px-St_Maarten_Hurricane_Damage.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3019" data-file-height="2443" /></a><figcaption>Ground view of Hurricane Irma's damage</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Irma" title="Hurricane Irma">Hurricane Irma</a> made landfall on 6 September 2017, causing extensive damage. 4 deaths were ultimately reported; there were 11 serious injuries out of a total of 34.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Princess Juliana Airport was extensively damaged but reopened on a partial basis in two days to allow incoming relief flights and for flights that would take evacuees to other islands.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> By 8 September, "many inhabitants [were] devoid of basic necessities" and looting had become a serious problem.<sup id="cite_ref-Davies_2017_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Davies_2017-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Reports on 9 September indicated that 70% of the infrastructure on the Dutch part had been destroyed.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> A survey by the Dutch Red Cross estimated that nearly a third of the buildings in Sint Maarten had been destroyed and that over 90 per cent of structures on the island had been damaged.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The prime minister of the Netherlands, <a href="/wiki/Mark_Rutte" title="Mark Rutte">Mark Rutte</a>, told the news media on 8 September that the airport in Sint Maarten was ready to receive emergency flights and that aid, as well as police officers and military personnel, were on their way.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The prime minister of Sint Maarten, <a href="/wiki/William_Marlin" title="William Marlin">William Marlin</a>, had already asked the Dutch government for extended relief assistance which began to arrive on 8 September. The government issued a tropical storm warning on 8 September since the Category 4 <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Jose_(2017)" title="Hurricane Jose (2017)">Hurricane Jose</a> was approaching.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government of the Netherlands was sending aid, as well as additional police and military, since looting was a serious problem. A statement by Marlin summarized the situation on 8 September. "We've lost many, many homes. Schools have been destroyed. We foresee a loss of the tourist season because of the damage that was done to hotel properties, the negative publicity that one would have that it's better to go somewhere else because it's destroyed. So that will have a serious impact on our economy."<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> At the time, preparations were being made as <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Jose_(2017)" title="Hurricane Jose (2017)">Hurricane Jose</a> approached the island.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Government estimates on 9 September indicated that 70 percent of houses were badly damaged or destroyed; much of the population was living in shelters pending the arrival of Jose. Thankfully, this second hurricane did not have a significant impact on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Widespread looting had started and a state of emergency was announced; some 230 soldiers from the Netherlands were patrolling. Additional Dutch troops were expected.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> By 10 September, some 1,200 Americans had been evacuated to <a href="/wiki/Puerto_Rico" title="Puerto Rico">Puerto Rico</a> from Sint Maarten by military aircraft during a time of looting and violence. On that date, <a href="/wiki/Royal_Caribbean_International" title="Royal Caribbean International">Royal Caribbean International</a> said that the company was sending its <a href="/wiki/Adventure_of_the_Seas" title="Adventure of the Seas">Adventure of the Seas</a> to Saint-Martin and to St. Thomas to provide supplies and to offer evacuation services.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The ship arrived on the island on 10 September with water, ice, garbage bags, clothing and canned food, and evacuated 320 people.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> By 11 September, King Willem-Alexander had already arrived in Curaçao and was scheduled to visit St. Maarten, St. Eustatius and Saba.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> When King Willem-Alexander visited Sint Maarten for the first time post-hurricane, he was shocked by the destruction. He immediately called for support from the European Union so the island could recover swiftly. Later in the month, it was revealed that the EU would allocate €2&#160;billion in emergency funds for immediate disaster relief to restore basic essentials on Sint Maarten, such as drinking water and sanitation.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> In addition to the EU's contribution, Red Cross, the government of the Netherlands, and Dutch citizens of the mainland pitched in (via donations and crowdfunding) to raise money for the devastated island.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Post_hurricane_rebuilding">Post hurricane rebuilding</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Post hurricane rebuilding">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>On 10 October 2017, Princess Juliana International Airport re-commenced commercial flights<sup id="cite_ref-SXM_Airport_2017_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SXM_Airport_2017-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> using temporary structures, pending repairs.<sup id="cite_ref-SXM_Airport_2018_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SXM_Airport_2018-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A report in late March 2018 indicated that the airport was able to handle some flights and some service had resumed from the US, Canada, and Europe. A new departure lounge was being used during rebuilding of the original facility. The General Aviation building was being used for passengers arriving on the island.
</p><p>A little over a year after Hurricane Irma, St Maarten's cruise industry had recovered to the extent that in 2018, more than 1&#160;million cruise passengers visited the island.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Telecommunications, including Wi-Fi, had been restored on the island, 95% of customers were receiving electricity and drinking water was readily available on the island. Some tourist accommodations were open, with 27 operating and 36 said to be ready sometime later this year. Cruise ships were arriving; a full 14 were accommodated the week of February 18, 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint-Martin_Island_topographic_map-en.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Saint-Martin_Island_topographic_map-en.svg/330px-Saint-Martin_Island_topographic_map-en.svg.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="249" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Saint-Martin_Island_topographic_map-en.svg/495px-Saint-Martin_Island_topographic_map-en.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Saint-Martin_Island_topographic_map-en.svg/660px-Saint-Martin_Island_topographic_map-en.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="2600" data-file-height="1960" /></a><figcaption>Detailed map showing Sint Maarten</figcaption></figure>
<p>Sint Maarten occupies the southern part of the island of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Martin_(island)" title="Saint Martin (island)">Saint Martin</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands" title="Leeward Islands">Leeward Islands</a>; the northern half forms the French territory of <a href="/wiki/Collectivity_of_Saint_Martin" title="Collectivity of Saint Martin">Saint Martin</a>. To the north across the <a href="/wiki/Anguilla_Channel" title="Anguilla Channel">Anguilla Channel</a> lies the British Overseas Territory of <a href="/wiki/Anguilla" title="Anguilla">Anguilla</a>, to the south-east of the island lies the French island of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Saint Barthélemy">Saint Barthélemy</a>, and further south are the Dutch islands of <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Saint_Eustatius" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Eustatius">Saint Eustatius</a>.
</p><p>Sint Maarten is 34&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (13&#160;sq&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The terrain is generally hilly, with the highest peak being <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mount_Flagstaff&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Mount Flagstaff (page does not exist)">Mount Flagstaff</a> at 383m.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The area to the west around the airport is flatter, and contains the Dutch section of the <a href="/wiki/Simpson_Bay_Lagoon" title="Simpson Bay Lagoon">Simpson Bay Lagoon</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Great_Salt_Pond,_Sint_Maarten" title="Great Salt Pond, Sint Maarten">Great Salt Pond</a> lies to north of Philipsburg. Several small islands lie off the coast. Little Key lies in the Simpson Bay Lagoon.
</p><p>There are ten total islands in Sint Maarten, including:<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

</div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Sint Maarten has a <a href="/wiki/Tropical_savanna_climate" title="Tropical savanna climate">tropical savanna climate</a> (<a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification" title="Köppen climate classification">Köppen</a> <i>Aw</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-Philipsburg,_Sint_Maarten_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Philipsburg,_Sint_Maarten-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> and is drier than most parts of the northeastern Caribbean because of a <a href="/wiki/Rain_shadow" title="Rain shadow">rain shadow</a> from the island's mountains, drying the <a href="/wiki/Trade_winds" title="Trade winds">trade winds</a>. The driest months are from January to July, and the wettest from September to November, when <a href="/wiki/Hurricane" class="mw-redirect" title="Hurricane">hurricanes</a> can strike the island.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Sint_Maarten" title="Politics of Sint Maarten">Politics of Sint Maarten</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png/330px-EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png/495px-EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png/660px-EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png 2x" data-file-width="2206" data-file-height="1358" /></a><figcaption>The European Union and the UK (prior to the latter's departure) in the world with overseas countries and territories and outermost regions</figcaption></figure>
<p>Sint Maarten is a constituent country of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a>, and as such the <a href="/wiki/Monarch_of_the_Netherlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Monarch of the Netherlands">monarch of the Netherlands</a> is head of state, represented locally by a <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Sint_Maarten" title="Governor of Sint Maarten">governor</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Following the dissolution of the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Sint_Maarten" title="Constitution of Sint Maarten">Constitution of Sint Maarten</a> was unanimously adopted by the island council of Sint Maarten on 21 July 2010. Elections for a new island council <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten_general_election,_2010" class="mw-redirect" title="Sint Maarten general election, 2010">were held on 17 September 2010</a>, since the number of seats was increased from 11 to 15. The newly elected island council became the <a href="/wiki/Estates_of_Sint_Maarten" class="mw-redirect" title="Estates of Sint Maarten">Estates of Sint Maarten</a> on 10 October.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Sint Maarten is largely autonomous in internal affairs, with the Netherlands responsible for foreign diplomacy and defence.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The first woman to be the president of Sint Maarten was <a href="/wiki/Gracita_Arrindell" title="Gracita Arrindell">Gracita Arrindell</a>, who was first elected in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There currently is a movement in Sint Maarten aiming for <a href="/wiki/Unification_of_Saint_Martin" title="Unification of Saint Martin">the unification of island of Saint Martin</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> which has its own flag.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_policy_and_defence">Foreign policy and defence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Foreign policy and defence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Kingdom of the Netherlands has overarching responsibility for foreign relations, defence and Dutch nationality law in the Caribbean parts of the Kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> A detachment of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Marine_Corps" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal Netherlands Marine Corps">Royal Netherlands Marine Corps</a> is present on Sint Maarten and the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Navy" title="Royal Netherlands Navy">Royal Netherlands Navy</a> deploys a guardship, normally a <a href="/wiki/Holland-class_offshore_patrol_vessel" title="Holland-class offshore patrol vessel"><i>Holland</i>-class</a> <a href="/wiki/Offshore_patrol_vessel" class="mw-redirect" title="Offshore patrol vessel">offshore patrol vessel</a>, in the Caribbean on a rotational basis together with the support vessel <a href="/wiki/HNLMS_Pelikaan_(A804)" title="HNLMS Pelikaan (A804)">HNLMS&#160;<i>Pelikaan</i></a> which operates out of Curacao.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean_Coast_Guard" title="Dutch Caribbean Coast Guard">Dutch Caribbean Coast Guard</a>, directed by the commander of the Royal Netherlands Navy in the Caribbean, operates throughout the Caribbean and is funded by the four constituent countries of the Kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environmental_laws">Environmental laws</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Environmental laws">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The beach policy (as of 1994)<sup id="cite_ref-Ministry_Policies_and_Reports_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ministry_Policies_and_Reports-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> views the beach from the perspective of being an ecosystem service for recreational activities. This is because the economy on Sint Maarten is tourism-driven and many tourists come to the island to enjoy the 37 beaches on the island. The policy has three main points: the beach must be usable for everyone, developments negatively affecting recreational use will be prevented, and beaches should be protected against human influences that could impair their recreational function. The policy's main purpose is to protect the recreational value of the beach. The laws do not consider the protection and ecological value of this habitat in regard to protecting nesting <a href="/wiki/Sea_turtle" title="Sea turtle">sea turtles</a>, preserving the beach line, or preserving the plants that live in and along the beach line.
</p><p>The hillside policy, as of 1998,<sup id="cite_ref-Ministry_Policies_and_Reports_50-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ministry_Policies_and_Reports-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> is mainly concerned with residential development. On the hillside, only residential development is permitted, certain hillsides with important “visual impact” are protected and conserved for their general landscape. A natural park is projected for the following hills: Cole Bay Hill, Sentry Hill, St. Peters Hill, Concordia Hill, Marigot Hill, Waymouth Hill, and Williams Hill. The policy stated the main objective was to conserve and maintain the green hillside and restore any natural habitats if needed. However, as of 2020, these natural parks have not yet been established.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Corruption">Corruption</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Corruption">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1978, the government of the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a> installed a Research Committee on the <a href="/wiki/Windward_Islands" title="Windward Islands">Windward Islands</a> (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>: <i lang="nl">Commissie van Onderzoek Bovenwindse Eilanden</i>) to investigate claims of corruption in the island government. Even though the report issued by this commission was damaging for the island's government, measures were not put into place to curb corruption, arguably because the government of the Netherlands Antilles depended on the support of Wathey's Democratic Party in the <a href="/wiki/Estates_of_the_Netherlands_Antilles" class="mw-redirect" title="Estates of the Netherlands Antilles">Estates of the Netherlands Antilles</a>. In August 1990, the public prosecutor of the Netherlands Antilles started an investigation into the alleged ties between the island government of Sint Maarten and the <a href="/wiki/Sicilian_Mafia" title="Sicilian Mafia">Sicilian Mafia</a>, and in 1991 the <a href="/wiki/Court_of_Audit" title="Court of Audit">Court of Audit</a> of the Netherlands Antilles issued a report which concluded that the island government of Sint Maarten was ailing.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the government and parliament of the Netherlands, the call for measures became louder. With Dutch pressure, the government of the Netherlands Antilles installed the <a href="/wiki/Miguel_Pourier" title="Miguel Pourier">Pourier</a> Commission tasked with investigating the state of affairs of the island government of Sint Maarten in December 1991. Its report concluded that the island was in a severe financial crisis, that rules of democratic decision-making were continuously broken, and that the island government constituted an <a href="/wiki/Oligarchy" title="Oligarchy">oligarchy</a>. In short, the island government failed completely according to the report. After long negotiations, the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_of_the_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Council of Ministers of the Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom government</a> enacted a General Measure of Kingdom Administration (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>: <i lang="nl">Algemene Maatregel van Rijksbestuur</i>) in early 1993, placing Sint Maarten under direct supervision of the Kingdom. Although originally meant for one year, the Order-in-Council for the Kingdom was eventually extended until 1 March 1996.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Though much has changed since, allegations of criminal activities continue to plague Sint Maarten. In 2004, the Minister of Justice of the Netherlands Antilles asked the Scientific Research and Documentation Centre (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>: <i lang="nl">Wetenschappelijk Onderzoek- en Documentatiecentrum</i> (WODC)) of the Dutch Ministry of Justice to conduct research into organized crime in Sint Maarten. The report concluded that <a href="/wiki/Money_laundering" title="Money laundering">money laundering</a> and cocaine trade are widespread on Sint Maarten. It also alleged that money from the island was used to finance <a href="/wiki/Hamas" title="Hamas">Hamas</a>, its associate <a href="/wiki/Holy_Land_Foundation" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Land Foundation">Holy Land Foundation</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Taliban" title="Taliban">Taliban</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In April 2009, former Commissioner Louie Laveist was convicted, and sentenced to an 18-month prison sentence, by the Sint Maarten Court-of-First-Instance, on account of forgery, fraud, and bribery.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> He was later acquitted of forgery and of fraud by the <a href="/wiki/Common_Court_of_Justice_of_the_Netherlands_Antilles_and_Aruba" class="mw-redirect" title="Common Court of Justice of the Netherlands Antilles and Aruba">Common Court of Justice of the Netherlands Antilles and Aruba</a>, but not of bribery.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Ecology">Ecology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Ecology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Plants">Plants</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Plants">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Sint Maarten is home to many distinctive plants such as <a href="/wiki/Hibiscus_rosa-sinensis" title="Hibiscus rosa-sinensis">hibiscus</a>, yellow sage (seen on the flag), <a href="/wiki/Delonix_regia" title="Delonix regia">flamboyant trees</a>, mahogany, and cacti. An estimated 522 wild plants are present, mainly being seed plants and a few ferns. The <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Calyptranthes_boldinghgii&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Calyptranthes boldinghgii (page does not exist)">Calyptranthes boldinghgii</a></i> and <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Galactia_nummelaria&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Galactia nummelaria (page does not exist)">Galactia nummelaria</a></i> are “<a href="/wiki/Island_endemic" class="mw-redirect" title="Island endemic">island-endemic</a>” and it is suspected that they have already gone extinct. Much of the hilltops are semi-evergreen seasonal forests which are rare in region.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The categorization of native, introduced, and invasive plant species is not as well documented for the island. Some of the introduced plant species include: manila grass (<i><a href="/wiki/Zoysia_matrella" title="Zoysia matrella">Zoysia matrella</a></i>), Spanish bayonet ( <a href="/wiki/Yucca_aloifolia" title="Yucca aloifolia">Y<i>ucca aloifolia</i></a>), Singapore almond (<i><a href="/wiki/Terminalia_catappa" title="Terminalia catappa">Terminalia catappa</a></i>), true aloe (<i><a href="/wiki/Aloe_vera" title="Aloe vera">Aloe vera</a></i>). Some of the native species are west Indian holly (<i><a href="/wiki/Turnera_ulmifolia" title="Turnera ulmifolia">Tunera ulmifolia</a></i>), spiny amaranth (<i><a href="/wiki/Amaranthus_spinosus" title="Amaranthus spinosus">Amaranthus spinosus</a></i>), bell pepper (C<i>apsium pulcherrima</i>), salt heliotrope (<i><a href="/wiki/Heliotropium_curassavicum" title="Heliotropium curassavicum">Heliotropium curassavicum</a></i>), bay rum tree (<i>pimento racemose</i>), and sourbush (<i>pluchea carolinesis</i>). One of the invasive species on the island is crowfoot grass (<i><a href="/wiki/Dactyloctenium_aegyptium" title="Dactyloctenium aegyptium">Dactyloctenium aegyptium</a></i>).<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mullet_Pond">Mullet Pond</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Mullet Pond">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Mullet Pond, a section of the inland lagoon <a href="/wiki/Simpson_Bay_Lagoon" title="Simpson Bay Lagoon">Simpson Bay Lagoon</a>, is home to 70% of Sint Maarten's mangrove population on the Dutch side of the island. <a href="/wiki/Mangrove" title="Mangrove">Mangroves</a> are a nursery for many young fish and during hurricane season they provide coastal protection. The area, however, is at risk due to dredging, tourism activities, and the yacht industry on the island.
</p><p>Mullet Pond is the 55th <a href="/wiki/Ramsar_site" title="Ramsar site">Ramsar site</a> since 2016<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> and therefore protected according to the Ramsar Treaty, a global commitment to protect ecologically significant wetland areas.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Challenges">Challenges</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Challenges">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The effects of <a href="/wiki/Global_warming" class="mw-redirect" title="Global warming">climate change</a> are felt on Sint Maarten. According to the Netherlands Antilles Coral Reef Initiative the coral reefs were fragmented due to a temperature rise to 30&#160;°C in 2005.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Twenty years ago, the sea grass beds were much larger. Natural disasters (hurricanes), development, and a tourism-based industry caused a significant decrease over the years. The <a href="/wiki/Seagrass_meadow" title="Seagrass meadow">seagrass beds</a> are important for anchoring the sand in place as well as hurricane protection.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Without the seagrass bed sand can easily be moved by a hurricane resulting in the loss of beaches or sand accumulates in one area, impacting marine life.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Philipsburg_%E2%80%93_Boardwalk_-_Catholic_Church_St._Martin_of_Tours_-_panoramio.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Philipsburg_%E2%80%93_Boardwalk_-_Catholic_Church_St._Martin_of_Tours_-_panoramio.jpg/220px-Philipsburg_%E2%80%93_Boardwalk_-_Catholic_Church_St._Martin_of_Tours_-_panoramio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Philipsburg_%E2%80%93_Boardwalk_-_Catholic_Church_St._Martin_of_Tours_-_panoramio.jpg/330px-Philipsburg_%E2%80%93_Boardwalk_-_Catholic_Church_St._Martin_of_Tours_-_panoramio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Philipsburg_%E2%80%93_Boardwalk_-_Catholic_Church_St._Martin_of_Tours_-_panoramio.jpg/440px-Philipsburg_%E2%80%93_Boardwalk_-_Catholic_Church_St._Martin_of_Tours_-_panoramio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3869" data-file-height="2992" /></a><figcaption>Catholic Church St. Martin of Tours in Philipsburg</figcaption></figure>
<p>In the 2011 Netherlands Antilles census, the population of the island territory was 33,609.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> In the 2017 census the total population of the country was 40,535.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Settlements">Settlements</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Settlements">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Philipsburg,_Sint_Maarten" title="Philipsburg, Sint Maarten">Philipsburg</a> (1,894 inhabitants)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Lower_Prince%27s_Quarter" title="Lower Prince&#39;s Quarter">Lower Prince's Quarter</a> (10,833 inhabitants)</li>
<li>Cul de Sac (8,588 inhabitants)</li>
<li>Cole Bay (7,194 inhabitants)</li>
<li>Upper Prince's Quarter (4,595 inhabitants)</li>
<li>Little Bay (Fort Amsterdam) (5,581 inhabitants)</li>
<li>Simpson Bay (1,142 inhabitants)</li>
<li>Lowlands (708 inhabitants)</li></ul>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="barbox tleft" style="overflow-x: auto;">
<div style="border:1px solid silver; font-size:88%; padding:0.4em; width:auto; background: white;">
<table style="text-align:left; border-collapse:collapse; width:100%;">
<tbody><tr style="background:#ddd"><th style="text-align:center;" colspan="5">Religions of Sint Maarten<sup id="cite_ref-CIAPAPUANEWGUINEA_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAPAPUANEWGUINEA-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup></th></tr>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="barbox tleft" style="overflow-x: auto;">
