<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a> is the everyday language of communication in Sint Maarten, and the first language of native Sint Maarteners.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> A local variety of <a href="/wiki/Virgin_Islands_Creole" title="Virgin Islands Creole">Virgin Islands Creole</a> is spoken in informal situations by Sint Maarteners between themselves. Though it holds official status, <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a> is learned by most Sint Maarteners as a second language, and generally used only when communicating with other Dutch speakers.
</p><p>The government uses Dutch when in communication with the national government and also formerly did so with the Netherlands Antilles government. Local signage uses both Dutch and English.<sup id="cite_ref-Klompp80_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Klompp80-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
Historically there were English-medium and Dutch-medium schools on Sint Maarten, but Dutch government policy towards St. Maarten and other SSS islands eventually promoted English-medium education.
</p><p>Sint Maarten is a <a href="/wiki/Multilingualism" title="Multilingualism">polyglot</a> society, most are <a href="/wiki/Simultaneous_bilingualism" title="Simultaneous bilingualism">simultaneously bilingual</a> in Dutch and English, and among them are also speakers of <a href="/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language">Spanish</a> and <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>, the latter of which is official on the other side of the island (<a href="/wiki/Collectivity_of_Saint_Martin" title="Collectivity of Saint Martin">Saint-Martin</a>). Linguist Linda-Andrea Richardson stated in 1983 that Dutch was a "<a href="/wiki/Dead_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Dead language">dead language</a>" in Sint Maarten.<sup id="cite_ref-Sociolinguisticsp2108_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sociolinguisticsp2108-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some residents, including Arubans and St. Martiners who lived in <a href="/wiki/Aruba" title="Aruba">Aruba</a>, speak <a href="/wiki/Papiamento" title="Papiamento">Papiamento</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Maarten_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Saint_Maarten_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Saint_Maarten_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Saint_Maarten_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Saint_Maarten_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Saint_Maarten_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Saint_Maarten_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a><figcaption>A proportional representation of Saint Maarten exports, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Maarten,_Dutch_Side.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Saint_Maarten%2C_Dutch_Side.jpg/220px-Saint_Maarten%2C_Dutch_Side.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="142" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Saint_Maarten%2C_Dutch_Side.jpg/330px-Saint_Maarten%2C_Dutch_Side.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Saint_Maarten%2C_Dutch_Side.jpg/440px-Saint_Maarten%2C_Dutch_Side.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2559" data-file-height="1655" /></a><figcaption>Many tourists come to use Sint Maarten's beaches.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Sint Maarten, along with <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Curaçao">Curaçao</a>, uses the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antillean_guilder" title="Netherlands Antillean guilder">Netherlands Antillean guilder</a> as its currency. The economy is heavily dependent on tourism, either from long-stays or day-trippers from the many <a href="/wiki/Cruise_line" title="Cruise line">cruise lines</a> that dock in the <a href="/wiki/Philipsburg,_Sint_Maarten" title="Philipsburg, Sint Maarten">Philipsburg</a> Harbour; around 80% of the workforce is employed in this sector.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Some limited agriculture occurs, however most food is imported.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_6-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2014, St. Maarten had more gaming machines per resident than any other country in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hurricane Irma severely affected the economy in 2017. In a 2019 report, it was revealed that the island's GDP had dropped by 4.7%, with an increase in inflation.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> This drastic hit to the economy was due to lessened tourism, real estate, trade, and business activities.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Saint_Martin" class="mw-redirect" title="Culture of Saint Martin">Culture of Saint Martin</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:CourthouseSXM.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/CourthouseSXM.png/220px-CourthouseSXM.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="238" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/CourthouseSXM.png/330px-CourthouseSXM.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/CourthouseSXM.png/440px-CourthouseSXM.png 2x" data-file-width="776" data-file-height="840" /></a><figcaption>Courthouse on Sint Maarten</figcaption></figure>
<p>Sint Maarten's culture is a mix of African, European and North American influences. Ank Klomp wrote in <i>Saint Martin: Communal Identities on a Divided Caribbean Island</i> that Sint Maarten lacked a Dutch cultural identity.<sup id="cite_ref-Klompp80_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Klompp80-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Festivals">Festivals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Festivals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>An annual <a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten_Heineken_Regatta&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Sint Maarten Heineken Regatta (page does not exist)">regatta</a> is held over three days culminating in the first weekend in March. Among the leading cultural artists of the island are Isidore "Mighty Dow" York (<a href="/wiki/Kaiso" title="Kaiso">kaisonian</a>, panman), Roland Richardson (<a href="/wiki/Impressionist" class="mw-redirect" title="Impressionist">Impressionist</a> painter), <a href="/wiki/Nicole_de_Weever" title="Nicole de Weever">Nicole de Weever</a> (dancer, broadway star), Ruby Bute (painter, storyteller, poet), Clara Reyes (choreographer), Susha Hien (choreographer), <a href="/wiki/Lasana_M._Sekou" title="Lasana M. Sekou">Lasana M. Sekou</a> (poet, author, independence advocate), <a href="/wiki/Drisana_Deborah_Jack" class="mw-redirect" title="Drisana Deborah Jack">Drisana Deborah Jack</a> (visual artist, poet), and Tanny and The Boys (string band music group). The annual St. Maarten Carnival starts in April and ends in May. The Grand Carnival parade that takes place on the Dutch side is the largest parade of the island's two carnivals. The annual St. Martin Book Fair takes place during the first weekend of June, featuring emerging and famous authors from the island, the Caribbean region, and from around the world.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Popular team sports in Sint Maarten include <a href="/wiki/Baseball" title="Baseball">baseball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cricket" title="Cricket">cricket</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Soccer" class="mw-redirect" title="Soccer">soccer</a>. <a href="/wiki/Recreational_fishing" title="Recreational fishing">Recreational fishing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Golf" title="Golf">golf</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Water_sports" class="mw-redirect" title="Water sports">water sports</a> (including <a href="/wiki/Underwater_diving" title="Underwater diving">diving</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kayaking" title="Kayaking">kayaking</a>, <a href="/wiki/Snorkelling" class="mw-redirect" title="Snorkelling">snorkelling</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Yachting" title="Yachting">yachting</a>) are popular amongst tourists.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten_Soccer_Association" class="mw-redirect" title="Sint Maarten Soccer Association">Sint Maarten Soccer Association</a> was founded in 1986. The organisation is not a member of <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a>, but became an associate member of <a href="/wiki/CONCACAF" title="CONCACAF">CONCACAF</a> in 2002, and a full member in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten_national_football_team" title="Sint Maarten national football team">The national football team</a> debuted in 1989, and plays its home games at the <a href="/wiki/Raoul_Illidge_Sports_Complex" title="Raoul Illidge Sports Complex">Raoul Illidge Sports Complex</a>, which has a 3,000-spectator capacity. After an initial period of popularity during the 1990s, including an appearance at the <a href="/wiki/1993_Caribbean_Cup" class="mw-redirect" title="1993 Caribbean Cup">1993 Caribbean Cup</a>, interest in football declined, with the national team playing its last official match in 2000 (against <a href="/wiki/Dominica_national_football_team" title="Dominica national football team">Dominica</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> However, Sint Maarten returned to international competition in March 2016, for the <a href="/wiki/2017_Caribbean_Cup_qualification" title="2017 Caribbean Cup qualification">2017 Caribbean Cup qualification</a> tournament.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Sint Maarten Cricket Association is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands_Cricket_Association" title="Leeward Islands Cricket Association">Leeward Islands Cricket Association</a> (LICA), which is, in turn, a member of the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies_Cricket_Board" class="mw-redirect" title="West Indies Cricket Board">West Indies Cricket Board</a> (WICB). With rare exceptions (for instance, the <a href="/wiki/Stanford_20/20" title="Stanford 20/20">Stanford 20/20</a>), <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten_national_cricket_team" title="Sint Maarten national cricket team">the national cricket team</a> plays only against other LICA members, though Sint Maarteners may go on to play for the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands_cricket_team" title="Leeward Islands cricket team">Leeward Islands team</a> at regional level and are eligible for both the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies_cricket_team" title="West Indies cricket team">West Indies</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_national_cricket_team" title="Netherlands national cricket team">Netherlands</a> internationally. The primary venue for cricket is the <a href="/wiki/Carib_Lumber_Ball_Park" title="Carib Lumber Ball Park">Charles Vlaun Cricket Field</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Colin_Hamer" title="Colin Hamer">Colin Hamer</a> was the first Sint Maartener to play <a href="/wiki/First-class_cricket" title="First-class cricket">first-class cricket</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> while <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Doram" title="Daniel Doram">Daniel Doram</a> was the first islander to play at international level, debuting for the Netherlands against Ireland in the <a href="/wiki/ICC_Intercontinental_Cup" title="ICC Intercontinental Cup">Intercontinental Cup</a> in July 2013 at the age of 15, also becoming the first St. Maartener to take a first-class five-wicket haul. In 2016 <a href="/wiki/Keacy_Carty" title="Keacy Carty">Keacy Carty</a> became the first St Maartener to play representative cricket for the West Indies (for the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies_under-19_cricket_team" title="West Indies under-19 cricket team">West Indies under-19s</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Carty was the man of the final at the <a href="/wiki/2016_Under-19_Cricket_World_Cup" title="2016 Under-19 Cricket World Cup">2016 Under-19 World Cup</a>, and was later described by the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Sint_Maarten" title="Prime Minister of Sint Maarten">prime minister</a>, <a href="/wiki/William_Marlin" title="William Marlin">William Marlin</a>, as having "brought the name of St Maarten to international acclaim".<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Prior to cricket becoming popular, baseball was preferred. No national team existed, although Sint Maarteners were eligible to play for the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles_national_baseball_team" title="Netherlands Antilles national baseball team">Netherlands Antilles baseball team</a> before its dissolution.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Several Sint Maarteners have passed through the <a href="/wiki/Baseball_in_the_United_States" title="Baseball in the United States">American baseball system</a>, playing at <a href="/wiki/College_baseball" title="College baseball">college level</a> or in the <a href="/wiki/Minor_League_Baseball" title="Minor League Baseball">minor leagues</a>. Allen Halley played college baseball for the <a href="/wiki/South_Alabama_Jaguars" title="South Alabama Jaguars">South Alabama Jaguars</a> and was drafted by the <a href="/wiki/Chicago_White_Sox" title="Chicago White Sox">Chicago White Sox</a> in the 30th round of the <a href="/wiki/1995_Major_League_Baseball_draft" title="1995 Major League Baseball draft">1995 draft</a>, reaching <a href="/wiki/Class_A-Advanced" class="mw-redirect" title="Class A-Advanced">Class A-Advanced</a> in the minor leagues. Three others, Rene Leveret, Marc Ramirez, and Rafael Skeete, were signed as <a href="/wiki/Free_agents" class="mw-redirect" title="Free agents">free agents</a> by <a href="/wiki/Major_League_Baseball" title="Major League Baseball">major league</a> teams during their careers, but played only in the minor leagues.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Sint Maarten Volleyball Association is part of the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Eastern_Caribbean_Volleyball_Association&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Eastern Caribbean Volleyball Association (page does not exist)">Eastern Caribbean Volleyball Association</a>, which hosts championship qualifiers with countries within its zone. Countries that are part of the ECVA are: Anguilla, Antigua, Bermuda, Virgin Islands, Dominica, Dutch St.Martin, French St.Martin, Grenada, Montserrat, Saba, St. Eustatius, St.Kitts, St.Lucia, and St. Vincent &amp; the Grenadines.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> Over the past 8 years, volleyball in St.Maarten on a National level has been developing and showing results. In 2016, the Sint Maarten Men's National Team went on to win the championship in their pool for the round 1 World Championship Qualifiers winning the gold along with many individual awards. The local awardees were; Nicholas Henrietta (Best Setter); Leonardo J Jeffers (Best Outside Hitter); Stephan Ellis (Best Middle); Allinton Augustine (Best Defence); Riegmar Valies Courtar (Best Opposite), and Riegmar Valies Courtar (Best Scorer) and <a href="/wiki/Most_valuable_player" title="Most valuable player">MVP</a> Most Valuable Player.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Popular_culture">Popular culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Popular culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Air_Caraibes_Airbus_A330-323X_Breidenstein-2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Air_Caraibes_Airbus_A330-323X_Breidenstein-2.jpg/220px-Air_Caraibes_Airbus_A330-323X_Breidenstein-2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Air_Caraibes_Airbus_A330-323X_Breidenstein-2.jpg/330px-Air_Caraibes_Airbus_A330-323X_Breidenstein-2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Air_Caraibes_Airbus_A330-323X_Breidenstein-2.jpg/440px-Air_Caraibes_Airbus_A330-323X_Breidenstein-2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="811" /></a><figcaption>Tourists watch a low flying plane over the beach</figcaption></figure>
<p>The island is famous for its <a href="/wiki/Runway" title="Runway">runway</a> at <a href="/wiki/Princess_Juliana_International_Airport" title="Princess Juliana International Airport">Princess Juliana International Airport</a>, in which <a href="/wiki/Landing" title="Landing">landing</a> aircraft pass within 35 metres (38&#160;yd) of <a href="/wiki/Maho_Beach" title="Maho Beach">Maho Beach</a> below, due to the close proximity of the runway to the ocean. The planes appear to land dangerously close to beach goers so the beach and airport have become a popular place for people to view aeroplane landings. In July 2017, a New Zealander died from head injuries after being propelled backwards from a jet engine blast.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sint Maarten is also known for its festive nightlife, expansive beaches, precious jewelry, traditional cuisines, and plentiful casinos.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Previously residents had to complete secondary studies in <a href="/wiki/Aruba" title="Aruba">Aruba</a> or <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Curaçao">Curaçao</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sociolinguisticsp2108_65-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sociolinguisticsp2108-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Prior to 1976 Sint Maarten had two secondary schools: the government secondary school John Phillips School and the Catholic secondary school Pastoor Nieuwen Huis School. Philips was both a MAVO/ETAO school while Huis was a MAVO school. The foundation <a href="/wiki/Stichting_Voortgezet_Onderwijs_van_de_Bovenwindse_Eilanden" title="Stichting Voortgezet Onderwijs van de Bovenwindse Eilanden">Stichting Voortgezet Onderwijs van de Bovenwindse Eilanden</a>, established on February 20, 1974,<sup id="cite_ref-Mainhist_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mainhist-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> was created as the neutral governing body for a new school created by the merger of Phillips and Huis schools.<sup id="cite_ref-Sundialhist_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sundialhist-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> MPC, the merged school, opened on August 17, 1976.<sup id="cite_ref-Mainhist_85-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mainhist-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/w/index.php?title=Caribbean_International_Academy&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Caribbean International Academy (page does not exist)">Caribbean International Academy</a> (CIA), founded in 2003 is a preparatory private boarding and day school on the island of St. Maarten. Catering to children from Kindergarten to Grade 12, CIA is also the only school offering Canadian/Ontario High School Diploma (OSSD) and 90% of their graduates go on to attend universities in Europe, Canada and the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Learning Unlimited Preparatory School (LUPS) is an American accredited institution, that established a Caribbean location in St.Maarten in 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The school is accredited by the <a href="/wiki/Southern_Association_of_Independent_Schools" title="Southern Association of Independent Schools">Southern Association of Independent Schools</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Southern_Association_of_Colleges_and_Schools" title="Southern Association of Colleges and Schools">Southern Association of Colleges and Schools</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Most residents who attend tertiary institutions do so in Curaçao or European Netherlands.<sup id="cite_ref-Klompp80_64-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Klompp80-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/American_University_of_the_Caribbean" title="American University of the Caribbean">American University of the Caribbean</a> School of Medicine (AUC), founded in 1978, was previously located on <a href="/wiki/Montserrat" title="Montserrat">Montserrat</a>. Because of the eruption of the <a href="/wiki/Soufri%C3%A8re_Hills_volcano" class="mw-redirect" title="Soufrière Hills volcano">Soufrière Hills volcano</a> in 1995, AUC moved its campus to St. Maarten later that year. A permanent campus was completed in 1998 in <a href="/w/index.php?title=Cupecoy&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Cupecoy (page does not exist)">Cupecoy</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/University_of_St._Martin" title="University of St. Martin">University of St. Martin</a> is located in Philipsburg. The <a href="/wiki/University_of_Sint_Eustatius_School_of_Medicine" class="mw-redirect" title="University of Sint Eustatius School of Medicine">University of Sint Eustatius School of Medicine</a>, founded in 1999, was previously located on <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">Sint Eustatius</a>. In September 2013, the University of Sint Eustatius moved its campus to Cole Bay, St. Maarten.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Philipsburg Jubilee Library in Philipsburg was the most prominent library in Sint Maarten.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> However, after Hurricane Irma hit the island in 2017, the library was forced to shut down. As of February&#160;2019<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, Philipsburg Jubilee Library still lacks the funding necessary for it to be rebuilt,<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> but has recently reopened in a temporary location until further notice.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transportation">Transportation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Transportation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Philipsburg" class="mw-redirect" title="Port of Philipsburg">Port of Philipsburg</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Air_France_Airbus_A340-200_(F-GLZJ)_on_short_finals_at_Princess_Juliana_Airport.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Air_France_Airbus_A340-200_%28F-GLZJ%29_on_short_finals_at_Princess_Juliana_Airport.jpg/220px-Air_France_Airbus_A340-200_%28F-GLZJ%29_on_short_finals_at_Princess_Juliana_Airport.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Air_France_Airbus_A340-200_%28F-GLZJ%29_on_short_finals_at_Princess_Juliana_Airport.jpg/330px-Air_France_Airbus_A340-200_%28F-GLZJ%29_on_short_finals_at_Princess_Juliana_Airport.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Air_France_Airbus_A340-200_%28F-GLZJ%29_on_short_finals_at_Princess_Juliana_Airport.jpg/440px-Air_France_Airbus_A340-200_%28F-GLZJ%29_on_short_finals_at_Princess_Juliana_Airport.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="800" /></a><figcaption>Landing at the airport</figcaption></figure><p>
Sint Maarten is served by <a href="/wiki/Princess_Juliana_International_Airport" title="Princess Juliana International Airport">Princess Juliana International Airport</a>, serving destinations across the Caribbean, North America and France and the Netherlands. It is well known for its very low <a href="/wiki/Final_approach_(aeronautics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Final approach (aeronautics)">final approach</a> landings close to a popular beach at the end of the runway.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Winair" title="Winair">Winair</a> has its headquarters on the grounds of the airport.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup></p><div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sint_Maarten&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.sintmaartengov.org/">Official website of the Sint Maarten government</a></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external autonumber" href="https://www.dcbd.nl/sites/www.dcbd.nl/files/documents/RojerKNAP96-33BioInv-StMaarten%5Beng%5D.pdf">[3]</a></li>

</div></td></tr><tr><td class="navbox-abovebelow" colspan="2"><div><b>N.B.:</b> Territories in <i>italics</i> are parts of transregional sovereign states or non-sovereign dependencies.
<p><sup>^</sup>These three form the <a href="/wiki/SSS_islands" title="SSS islands">SSS islands</a> that with the ABC islands comprise the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean" title="Dutch Caribbean">Dutch Caribbean</a>, of which <sup>*</sup>the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">BES islands</a> are not direct <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom</a> constituents but subsumed with the country of the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands" title="Netherlands">Netherlands</a>.
</p><p><sup>†</sup><a href="/wiki/Physical_geography" title="Physical geography">Physiographically</a>, these <a href="/wiki/Island#Continental_islands" title="Island">continental islands</a> are not part of the volcanic Windward Islands arc, although sometimes grouped with them culturally and politically.
</p><p><sup>ǂ</sup><a href="/wiki/Territorial_dispute" title="Territorial dispute">Disputed territories</a> administered by <a href="/wiki/Guyana" title="Guyana">Guyana</a>. <sup>~</sup><a href="/wiki/Territorial_dispute" title="Territorial dispute">Disputed territories</a> administered by <a href="/wiki/Colombia" title="Colombia">Colombia</a>.
</p>
<sup>#</sup><a href="/wiki/Bermuda" title="Bermuda">Bermuda</a> is an isolated <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean" title="Atlantic Ocean">North Atlantic</a> <a href="/wiki/Island#Oceanic_islands" title="Island">oceanic island</a>, physiographically not part of the Lucayan Archipelago, Antilles, Caribbean Sea nor North American continental nor South American continental islands. It is grouped with the <a href="/wiki/North_America" title="North America">Northern American</a> region, but occasionally also with the Caribbean region culturally.</div></td></tr></tbody></table></div>
